```
    return best.tolist()
```

### Block mass optimization in detail

Since the exact expanded score for block vector `a` is almost `block_level_score(a)` times factor `(n/m)/B`? We can just optimize `block_level_score(a) = 2 * len(a) * max(conv(a)) / sum(a)^2`. Scale invariant. But exact score for expanded length N with block lengths L_j will be close. For initial search, use block_level to be fast. At end evaluate expanded.

The optimal block vector may have negative? We restrict positive. Could zeros be beneficial? The problem allows zero; for block masses, a zero mass block reduces effective m? If a_j=0, block level sequence has zero, max may lower? But objective factor m maybe worse. We can allow small epsilon.

For m=4, we can find optimum numerically using grid/gradient. Our manual vector may be near. But use optimizer.

Implement `optimize_block_masses(m, deadline, rng, target_score=None)`:
- Use a population of candidates and local refine.
- For each iteration batch, try multiple random starts until time.
- A random start can be generated by sampling shape parameters:
   * Dirichlet with alpha patterns: uniform, peaked [5,1,5,1], nonmonotone [2,1,3,1], etc.
   * polynomial shaping: a_i ∝ (1 + c*cos(2πi/m + phase))_+; or (1 - i/(m-1))^p; or two polynomials.
   * combination of low-dimensional known: for m=4 use candidate we found and random perturb.
- Local refine: `gradient_block(a, iterations, deadline)`.

Gradient block:
```
def block_max_active_gradient(a):
    c = np.convolve(a,a)
    M = c.max()
    # active mask: c >= M - max(1e-12, 1e-5*M) maybe top few. To avoid too many, choose top K = min(len(c), max(1, int(0.2*len(c))))? Or c > M - 1e-3*M.
    tol = max(1e-12, 1e-4*M)
    mask = c >= max(0, M - tol)
    # If too many, choose top P sorted.
    if mask.sum() > 7:
       idx = np.argsort(c)[-7:]; mask = zeros; mask[idx]=True
    g = np.zeros_like(a)
    for k in np.nonzero(mask)[0]:
        i0 = max(0,k-(len(a)-1)); i1 = min(len(a)-1,k)
        # g[i] += 2*a[k-i] for i=i0..i1
        g[i0:i1+1] += 2*a[k-i0:k-i0+1][::-1]? Need careful:
```
Let's derive: for given k, g[i] = 2*a[k-i] if 0≤k-i<m. So i ranges max(0,k-(m-1)) to min(m-1,k). For each i, a[k-i]. The slice `a[k - (i0:i1+1)]` is indices k-i0 down to k-i1 (descending). We need corresponding to ascending i; use `a[k - i0 : k - i1 - 1 : -1]`. E.g. m=4,k=1: i0=0,i1=1, a[1: -1:-1] = [a1,a0], g[0]=2a1,g[1]=2a0. OK. Sum over active. Then divide by active count.

```
loga = np.log(a + 1e-300)

```

```

for step in [1e-4,3e-4,1e-3,3e-3,1e-2,3e-2]:
   cand = np.exp(loga - step * g / scale)
```
Use line search. If no improvement, reduce scale or add small noise: `a = a + rng.normal(0,0.01); positive normalize`.

But block_score non-smooth; line search raw gradient may reduce? We can also use a simple coordinate/grid local improvement:
   For each coordinate i, perform a line search over ratio of a_i to others (normalize). Since objective piecewise quadratic; we can find min along 1D by sampling or using scipy `minimize_scalar`. For m small, we can do coordinate descent robust.

Example for varying coordinate i, set new vector with a_i = a_i * (1+t), others scaled to maintain sum: `b = a.copy(); b[i] *= (1+t); b /= b.sum()`. t in (-1, large). ### Full-length local search

We can design a smoother objective using log-sum-exp to avoid plateau non-differentiability. Let `c = conv(a)` length 2N-1. Define `F = logsumexp(c / tau) + log(const)`. Gradient wrt a: (1/tau) * softmax(c/tau) cross-correlation. This encourages reducing all large coefficients. Since sum fixed, minimize F approximates max. Use tau decreasing. Start with tau = 0.1*M? Need scale. c ~1/N ~0.00026. M~0.000184? Wait for normalized sum1, c values ~M_block/L ~1.8e-4. tau should be similar. Use tau = max(1e-8, 0.1*M) initially? If M~1e-4, tau~1e-5, softmax over c differences. If tau too small, numerics. Use `c_scaled = (c - M)/tau + 1`? To avoid overflow, subtract M. `weights = np.exp((c - M)/tau)`, normalize. Gradient as cross-corr. Multiply by 1/tau? If using weights normalized, gradient direction only; step can be tuned. We don't need exact scale.

For full-length, active weights may be too concentrated on one lag; we can use tau = M * alpha where alpha maybe 0.05 (5% below max). Later reduce alpha to 0.01 to focus. We can also use raw max subgradient if tau small.

Full search procedure from best sequence length N:
- Normalize `a` to sum1. Clip small to eps (e.g., 1e-12) to avoid log zero.
- For time:
   - Compute `c, M` via `convolve_normalized` maybe direct for small. Use FFT for N>1000.
   - Choose alpha sequence: start 0.2, then 0.1, 0.05, 0.02, maybe adapt: if no improvement for 200 iters, reduce alpha by half.
   - `weights = np.exp(np.clip((c - M)/ (alpha*M), -50, 0)); weights /= weights.sum()`
   - Compute gradient direction `g = cross_corr(a, weights) * 2`? But weights are probability over lags normalized; this gives mean subgradient. Note `weights` decays below M.
   - We need to normalize `a` after update. Log update: `loga = np.log(a); loga -= step * (g / g_max)`; `a_new = exp; a_new = clip; normalize`. Try steps.
   - Evaluate score fast; if improves, accept; else reduce step.
   - Occasionally (every 500) perform random block perturbations? But block start already.
   - To avoid getting stuck, if no improvement for many iterations, add noise: `a = a + sigma*rng.normal`; normalize; or restart from best with different noise.
- Since full N=3809 and starting from block plateaus, gradient will attempt to equalize. Multiplicative update may smooth plateaus. Could be effective.

But we must ensure `a` values remain not too large (≤1000). Normalized sum1, values ≤1 <1000, okay.

Potential issue with log update: If `g` has positive large at positions, exp(-step*g) reduces them; but all g positive? Weights positive and a positive, so g positive everywhere? For active lags, g_i =2∑ weights_k a_{k-i}; if weights for all lags positive (softmax over all 2N-1), g positive everywhere, log update reduces all coordinates, relative effect on coordinates with fewer active contributions less. Then normalization increases all. This is like mirror descent on penalized objective. Could work.

### Block lengths construction

`expand_blocks(N, masses)`:
- masses nonnegative sum1. We need assign integer lengths sum N.
- Use largest remainder round: `base = np.floor(N*masses)`, distribute remaining to largest fractional parts. Ensure each length at least 1 if mass >0? If mass 0 length 0 allowed? Sequence length must N total, zero-length block skipped. But if mass>0 and length 0, value infinite? avoid. For m up to 64 and N=3809, all masses positive enough length≥1? If some mass small <1/N, floor 0. We can set minimum length 1 for positive masses by allocating; adjust. Simpler: `lengths = np.maximum(1, np.rint(N*masses))`; then adjust to sum N by adding/subtracting from largest. But if mass* N <0.5, length1 might be too large relative mass; value huge (mass/1) but sum still, could increase peaks. Better use largest remainder exact: length may be 0 for tiny mass; then we can set that mass 0 to reduce m? For block optimization, avoid extremely small masses. If we do full sequence, zeros okay. #### FFT convolution
```
def _conv_full(a):
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0: return np.array([])
    if n <= 512:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    L = 1
    need = 2*n - 1
    while L < need:
        L <<= 1
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)
    return c[:2*n-1]
```
Use `float` arrays. For positive values, c positive. Due round-off, max might be slightly negative? No.

#### Cross-correlation direction
```
def _direction_from_lambda(a, lam):
    # lam array length 2n-1 nonnegative, sum maybe 1
    n=len(a)
    rb = lam[::-1]
    need = n + len(rb) - 1  # 3n-2
    L=1; while L<need: L<<=1
    FA=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    FB=np.fft.rfft(rb,L)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(FA*FB,L)[:need]
    # g[i] = conv[2n-2 - i]
    return conv[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```
Need ensure conv indices within need: max need =3n-2, 2n-2 < need for n>0. For n=1, need=2, conv indices? n-1:2*[0:1] works? 2n-2=0, n-1=0, slice 0:1. Good.
This direction is ∑_j a_j lam_{i+j}. If lam normalized, direction positive.

#### Full local search
```
def local_search_full(a, score0, deadline, rng, verbose=1):
    a = np.clip(a,0,None).astype(float); s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return a, inf
    a = a/s
    best = a.copy(); best_score = min(score0, score_array(a))
    M = max(_conv_full(a))
    # log base
    eps = 1e-300
    alpha = 0.1
    step = 0.1
    stall = 0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = _conv_full(a); M = float(c.max())
        if not finite or M<=0: break
        sigma = max(1e-15, alpha*M)
        # weights = exp((c-M)/sigma), normalized. To avoid underflow, clip -30
        z = (c - M)/sigma
        z = np.clip(z, -30, 0)
        w = np.exp(z)
        wsum = w.sum()
        if wsum <=0: w = np.zeros_like(c); idx = np.argmax(c); w[idx]=1
        else: w /= wsum
        d = _direction_from_lambda(a, w)
        # If direction has zeros? normalize by max
        dmax = float(d.max())
        if dmax <=0: break
        d = d / dmax
        improved = False
        # candidate steps
        for mult in [0.5,1.0,2.0,0.2,4.0,0.1,8.0,1.5,0.05,3.0]:
            st = step*mult
            ratios = np.exp(-st*d)  # d in [0,1], if st up to 8 ratio min 0.0003
            cand = a * ratios
            cs = cand.sum()
            if cs <= 1e-12: continue
            cand /= cs
            sc = score_array(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                best = cand.copy(); best_score=sc; a=cand; step=min(10, st*1.05) maybe; improved=True; break
        if improved:
            stall=0
            alpha = max(0.005, alpha*0.998?) # slowly tighten
        else:
            step *=0.5
            stall +=1
            if stall > 50:
                # increase alpha (smooth) or restart small noise
                alpha = min(0.5, alpha*1.5)
                if stall > 120:
                    # add noise to a
                    noise = rng.normal(0, 0.01 / a.size, a.size)
                    cand = a + noise
                    cand = np.clip(cand,0,None); cs=cand.sum(); if cs>... cand/=cs
                    sc=score_array(cand)
                    if sc < best_score: a=cand; best=...
                    else: a = best.copy()
                    stall=0
        it +=1
        if it % 200 ==0: print progress
    return best, best_score
```
Need be careful: If step becomes too small, no progress. We can have schedule: start step=0.1, alpha=0.1. The objective scale c ~1/N; direction normalized to max1, ratios exp(-step*d). Step dimensionless. Good.
The full score may not improve much because starting near optimum? okay.

Potential issue: The direction computed from *all* lags with weights decaying; if alpha too small, direction concentrated on max lag and update may explode one lag but worsen others. Alpha adaptive.

Another issue: `score_array` uses FFT; in line search, multiple candidate scores each recompute conv. Since candidate sequences differ by ratios, we could compute max via FFT; okay. But 10 steps * many iterations could be many. We can limit steps list shorter: [0.1,0.3,0.6,1.0,2.0,4.0]. Use first improving. Maybe step parameter adapt. Use 6 FFTs per iter; okay.

Can we use the active weights from current `c` to evaluate candidate's max more cheaply? Not necessary.

Potential performance: N=3809, FFT length for conv 8192? Need 2n-1=7617 -> L=8192. Cross corr need=3n-2=11425 -> L=16384. Per iteration: current conv (8192) + cross (16384) + up to 6 candidate convs (8192). 7 FFTs length ~8-16k, maybe 0.002s each? ~0.02s, 1800s -> 90k iterations. But Python loops and memory alloc ~ may be 0.05s, 36k iterations. Fine.

But if we run full search after block search, maybe 1000-5000 iterations enough. We don't need all time, but deadline. We can allow until 3s before end.

```
def optimize_block_masses(m, N, time_limit, rng):
```

Gradient block loop:
```
def block_loop(a, deadline, rng, verbose=False):
    a=normalize_positive(a, min=1e-8)
    best=a; best_sc=block_level_score(a)
    step=0.1; stall=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
       c=np.convolve(a,a); M=max(c)
       # active weights maybe softmax with alpha=0.05*M
       sigma=max(1e-15,0.05*M)
       w=np.exp(np.clip((c-M)/sigma,-30,0)); normalize
       # direction d_i = sum_j a_j w_{i+j}, same cross_corr but small can direct:
       d=np.zeros(m)
       # direct O(m*len(c)) okay m<=64 -> 8192 ops
       for k, wk in enumerate(w):
          if wk==0: continue
          i0=max(0,k-(m-1)); i1=min(m-1,k)
          d[i0:i1+1] += wk * a[k-i0:k?][::-1]
       dmax=max(d); d/=dmax
       for st in [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0]:
           cand = a*np.exp(-st*d); normalize; sc=block_level_score(cand)
           if sc<best_sc: accept
       ...
    return best
```
Direct direction O(m*2m) using loops over k maybe 16*32=512, fine. Could vectorize but not necessary. For m<=32.

But block_level_score uses direct convolve O(m²); fine.

Then exact expand. We can also use exact expanded score for gradient direction? Not necessary.

Time allocation: fallback constructed and scored. Then block optimizer for m=4,5,6,8 maybe 50s each. Then full local search with remaining. Use prints.

Let's design a custom block search more deterministic:

For a given m, we can perform "simulated annealing" over softmax parameters p:
- Represent a=softmax(p) (positive). Also allow zero? not needed.
- Objective `F(p)=2m*max(conv(softmax(p)))`.
- Use a mix of random restarts and coordinate perturbations.
- For each restart, run a local multiplicative gradient in probability space as earlier.
- Use time-limited.

The gradient in probability space can be computed as subgradient of max conv: g_i = average 2 a_{k-i}. We can update a multiplicatively `a *= exp(-eta*g)` then normalize. This is equivalent to p update plus renormalization. It should descend.

But softmax parameter and multiplicative update same after normalization. We can just work with a.

We need avoid local minima. We can generate many random starts and use short gradient refinements. For m up to 16, each iteration O(m²) convolve; very fast (m=16 -> 32 coefficients). We can do thousands.

Let's write `block_refine(a, deadline, rng, verbose_tag)`:
```
a = normalize_positive(a)
best=a; best_sc=block_level_score(a)
eta=0.2; alpha=0.05; stall=0; it=0
while time<deadline:
   c=np.convolve(a,a); M=max(c); sc=2*m*M
   # weights alpha
   sigma=max(1e-15, alpha*M)
   w=np.exp(np.clip((c-M)/sigma, -30, 0)); w/=w.sum()
   # direct d
   d=np.zeros(m)
   for k, wk in enumerate(w):
       if wk==0: continue
       i0=max(0,k-m+1); i1=min(m-1,k)
       # d[i] += wk * 2*a[k-i]? The direction normalized; include factor 2 not matter
       d[i0:i1+1] += wk * a[k - i0 : k - i1 -1 : -1]
   dmax=d.max(); if dmax==0 break; d/=dmax
   improved=False
   for mult in [0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0,0.1,3.0]:
      st=eta*mult
      cand=a*np.exp(-st*d)
      cand=np.clip(cand,0,None); s=cand.sum(); if s<eps: continue
      cand/=s
      sc2=block_level_score(cand)
      if sc2<best_sc-1e-12:
          best=cand; best_sc=sc2; a=cand; eta=min(2, st*1.05); improved=True; break
   if improved: stall=0; alpha=max(0.005, alpha*0.999)
   else: eta*=0.5; stall+=1; if stall>20: alpha=min(0.3, alpha*1.5); if stall>50: a=best*(1+rng.normal(0,0.02,m)); a=normalize; stall=0
   it++
return best
```
Note: The gradient d from weights includes `a` contributions; for active max lag, if `d` high, reducing those coordinates. Since we normalize, relative changes.

This should find near-optimal block vectors. But the objective has gauge symmetry (reversal), and gradient may get stuck at plateaus. Random restarts help.

Start generation for m:
```
def random_block_start(m, rng):
    mode=rng.integers(0,8)
    if mode==0: a=np.ones(m)
    elif mode==1: a=np.linspace(1,0.1,m) # decreasing
    elif mode==2: a=np.linspace(0.1,1,m) # increasing
    elif mode==3: a=(1+0.8*np.cos(2*np.pi*np.arange(m)/m + phase))**2 clipped
    elif mode==4: a=np.abs(1+0.8*np.sin(...))
    elif mode==5: a=rng.dirichlet alpha patterns
    ...
    add positive noise
```

### A connection to nonnegative "low autocorrelation" via "maximum entropy"

We can solve a master equation: At optimum, for active lags, gradient 2 conv shift. Multiplicative updates are proportional scaling: `a_i^{new}=a_i * exp(-η ∑ active a_{k-i})`. This resembles finding a potential such that `a_i` inversely proportional to exposure. Maybe we can use an Alternating Least Squares: If active lags known and we want c_k = M, the equations are linear in a? No, quadratic. But if we fix one factor of each pair, can update other minimally? For each active k, q_k = ∑ a_i a_{k-i} ≤M. We can model as a bipartite graph of pairs. Maybe use a "water-filling" where each coordinate participates in active constraints; if a constraint tight, scale down all involved weights by factor. But no.

```
def softmax(z):
    z = np.asarray(z,float)
    z = z - z.max()
    p = np.exp(z)
    p /= p.sum()
    return p

def block_objective_from_z(z, m):
    a=softmax(z); return block_level_score(a)
```
For DE, bounds `[[-3,3]]*m`. But softmax dimension m; uniform at z=0. It can produce a with min prob e^{-6}/m ≈0.00025/m? For m=16 ~1.6e-5, negligible. Good.

#### _convolution using FFT
```
def _convolution(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([], dtype=float)
    if n <= 600:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    need=2*n-1
    L=1
    while L < need:
        L <<= 1
    F=np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out=np.fft.irfft(F*F, L)
    out = out[:need]
    # due roundoff set tiny negative to 0
    # out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    return out
```
Use `np.maximum(out,0)` but if negative due to valid? For positive a, okay.

#### _score
```
def _score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.inf
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-12: return np.inf
    c=_convolution(a)
    if c.size==0: return np.inf
    M=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*M/(s*s))
```
This matches eval for nonnegative. For values clipped? We keep within.

#### _normalize
```
def _normalize(a, min_val=0.0):
    a=np.clip(np.asarray(a,dtype=float),0.0,1000.0)
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    s=a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-15:
       return np.ones_like(a)/a.size
    return a/s
```
For full search we want sum1.

#### _block_level_score
```
def _block_level_score(a):
    a=_normalize(a)
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    M=max(c)
    return 2.0*a.size*M # sum=1
```
Assumes sum1. If not, divide.

#### _expand_blocks
```
def _expand_blocks(N, masses):
    masses=np.asarray(masses,float); masses=np.clip(masses,0,None); S=masses.sum()
    if S <=0: return np.ones(N)/N
    masses/=S
    m=masses.size
    # lengths: start all 1? Use floor(N*mass) with min 1 if mass>1e-12
    lengths = np.floor(N*masses).astype(int)
    # ensure positive masses get at least 1 length
    pos = masses > 1e-12
    if pos.any():
        lengths[pos] = np.maximum(lengths[pos], 1)
    # adjust sum
    diff = N - lengths.sum()
    if diff >0:
        # add to largest masses
        order=np.argsort(-masses)
        idx=0
        while diff>0:
            lengths[order[idx%m]] +=1
            diff-=1; idx+=1
    elif diff<0:
        # remove from smallest positive lengths while >1
        order=np.argsort(masses)
        # keep lengths at least 1 for pos, but we can reduce total by 0? lengths sum >N due min; need remove.
        # If lengths > N, set lengths[pos]=1? We'll adjust.
        order=np.argsort(-lengths) # largest first to remove
        for j in order:
            if diff==0: break
            can=lengths[j]- (1 if masses[j] > 1e-12 and N >= m else 0) # if positive? maybe at least 1
            take=min(can, -diff)
            lengths[j] -= take
            diff += take
        # in rare case still, fill tail
        while diff<0:
            j=(...); lengths[j]-=1; diff+=1
    # values
    out=np.empty(N)
    start=0
    for val, L in zip(masses, lengths):
        if L>0:
            out[start:start+L] = val/L
            start += L
    # if start<N due issues, fill remaining with previous or zero
    if start<N:
       out[start:]=0.0
    elif start>N:
       out=out[:N]
    return out
```
Simpler lengths assignment: use largest remainder without min initially:
```
base=(N*masses).astype(int)
rem = int(N-base.sum())
# distribute rem to largest fractional; then ensure if mass>0 and length0 set length1 borrowing.
```
Let's implement robust:
```
def _integer_lengths(N, weights):
    w=np.asarray(weights,float); w=np.maximum(w,0); s=w.sum()
    if s<=0: return np.ones(N,dtype=int)
    w/=s
    base=np.floor(N*w).astype(np.int64)
    frac=N*w-base
    # distribute N-base.sum()
    diff=int(N-base.sum())
    order=np.argsort(-frac)
    i=0
    while diff>0:
       base[order[i%m]] +=1
       diff-=1; i+=1
    while diff<0:
       # remove from largest base
       j=int(np.argmax(base)); base[j]-=1; diff+=1
    # ensure each positive weight gets at least 1
    pos = w > 1e-12
    if np.any(pos & (base==0)):
       # set 0 to 1, borrow from largest >1
       zero_idx=np.nonzero(pos & (base==0))[0]
       for j in zero_idx:
          base[j]=1
       # fix sum
       while base.sum()>N:
          # remove from largest base where base> (1 if w>1e-12 else 0)
          candidates=base - np.where(pos,1,0)
          j=int(np.argmax(candidates))
          if candidates[j]<=0: break
          base[j]=candidates[j]-1? Actually base[j]-=1
       while base.sum()<N:
```

```
def _random_block_start(m, rng):
    mode = int(rng.integers(0,10))
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m,endpoint=False)
    if mode==0: a=np.ones(m)
    elif mode==1: a=1.0 - x + 0.1
    elif mode==2: a=x +0.1
    elif mode==3:
       phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi); amp=rng.choice([0.3,0.6,0.9,1.2])
       a=np.maximum(0.05, 1.0 + amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*x + phase))
    elif mode==4:
       phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi); amp=rng.choice([0.3,0.6,0.9])
       a=np.maximum(0.05, 1.0 + amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*x + phase))
    elif mode==5:
       # mix gaussians at two places
       c1=rng.uniform(0,1); c2=rng.uniform(0,1); w1=rng.uniform(0,1); w2=rng.uniform(0,1)
       a=w1*np.exp(-w1/10? ) ...
    else:
       alpha=rng.choice([0.2,0.5,1,2,5,10])
       # random profile
       a=np.maximum(1e-3, rng.gamma(alpha, np.ones(m)))
    a += rng.normal(0,0.05,a.size)
    return _normalize(a)
```

```
def _block_direction(a, weights):
    m=a.size
    d=np.zeros(m)
    for k, wk in enumerate(weights):
       if wk ==0: continue
       i0=max(0,k-m+1); i1=min(m-1,k)
       # indices in a: k-i0 down to k-i1
       d[i0:i1+1] += wk * a[k - i0 : k - i1 - 1 : -1]
    return d
```

#### _full_refine
Use top-level helpers. Need score function uses FFT.

Pseudo:
```
def _full_refine(a, rng, deadline):
    a=np.array(a,float); a=np.clip(a,0,None); s=a.sum(); if s<=... return a,inf
    a/=s
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=_score(best)
    step=0.2
    alpha=0.05
    stall=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline:
        c=_convolution(a)
        M=float(c.max())
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M<=1e-300: break
        sigma=max(1e-300, alpha*M)
        z=(c-M)/sigma
        z=np.clip(z, -30, 0)
        w=np.exp(z)
        ws=w.sum()
        if ws<=0: w=np.zeros_like(c); w[np.argmax(c)]=1
        else: w=1/ws*w
        d=_direction_from_weights(a,w)
        dmax=float(d.max())
        if dmax<=0: break
        d=d/dmax
        improved=False
        # choose steps
        for mult in (0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0,0.1,4.0,0.05,3.0):
            st=step*mult
            ratios=np.exp(-np.clip(st*d,0.0,50.0))
            cand=a*ratios
            cs=cand.sum()
            if cs<=1e-12: continue
            cand=Cand/cs
            sc=_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc<best_sc-1e-12:
                best=cand.copy(); best_sc=sc; a=cand
                step=min(5.0, st*1.05)
                improved=True
                break
        if improved:
            stall=0
            alpha=max(0.005, alpha*0.998)
        else:
            step*=0.7
            stall+=1
            if stall==20:
                alpha=min(0.3, alpha*1.5)
            if stall>50:
                # escape: perturb best
                cand=best.copy()
                # random multiplicative noise
                noise=rng.normal(0,0.1,cand.size)
                cand*=np.exp(noise)
                cand=_normalize(cand)
                sc=_score(cand)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc<best_sc:
                    a=cand; best=cand; best_sc=sc
                else:
                    a=best.copy()
                stall=0
                step=0.1
        it+=1
        if it%200==0: print(f"  full iter {it} best {best_sc:.6f}", flush=True)
    return best,best_sc
```
The direction `d` for full length with weights normalized over all lags. The gradient of logsumexp would have factor 1/sigma, but we normalize by dmax so step dimensionless. Good.

Could improve by using exact `evaluate_sequence` occasionally? Not needed.

### Detailed helper implementations

#### `_normalize_positive(a)`
```
def _normalize_positive(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=float).copy()
    if a.size == 0: return a
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a[a < 0.0] = 0.0
    a[a > 1000.0] = 1000.0
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=float) / max(1, a.size)
    return a / s
```

#### `_softmax`
```
def _softmax(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=float)
    if z.size == 0: return z
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    p = np.exp(z)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s): return np.ones(z.size)/z.size
    return p/s
```

#### `_resample`
```
def _resample(a, n):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    m=a.size
    if m==0: return np.ones(n,n?)/n
    if m==1: return np.ones(n)/n
    if m==n: return a
    xs=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m,endpoint=False)
    xn=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n,endpoint=False)
    b=np.interp(xn, xs, a)
    return _normalize_positive(b)
```

#### `_random_block_start`
Add antisymmetric start modes.
```
def _random_block_start(m, rng):
    mode=int(rng.integers(0,12))
    x=np.arange(m, dtype=float)
    cc=(m-1)/2.0
    if mode==0: a=np.ones(m)
    elif mode==1: a=np.linspace(1.0,0.1,m)
    elif mode==2: a=np.linspace(0.1,1.0,m)
    elif mode==3:
       phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi); amp=rng.choice([0.3,0.6,0.9,1.2])
       a=1.0 + amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*x/m + phase)
    elif mode==4:
       phase=rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi); amp=rng.choice([0.3,0.6,0.9,1.2])
       a=1.0 + amp*np.sin(2*np.pi*x/m + phase)
    elif mode==5:
       # antisymmetric random
       h=np.zeros(m)
       for i in range(m//2):
           v=rng.normal(0.0,1.0); h[i]=v; h[m-1-i]=-v
       scale=float(np.linalg.norm(h)*np.sqrt(m))
       if scale>0:
          h=h*8.0/scale
       amp=rng.choice([0.2,0.4,0.7,1.0])
       a=1.0 + amp*h
    elif mode==6:
       # Gaussian antisymmetric
       sigma=m*rng.uniform(0.15,0.45)
       g=(x-cc)/sigma
       h=g*np.exp(-g*g)
       # make antisymmetric? h is odd around center.
       a=1.0 + rng.choice([0.3,0.6,1.0])*h
    elif mode==7:
       alpha=rng.choice([0.2,0.5,1.0,3.0,8.0])
       a=rng.gamma(alpha, np.ones(m))
    elif mode==8:
       alpha=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(m)*rng.choice([0.2,0.5,1,3,10]))
       a=alpha
    else:
       # mix of two random motifs
       a=np.maximum(0.1, rng.normal(1.0,0.4,m))
    a=np.maximum(1e-4, a)
    if rng.random()<0.2:
        a += rng.normal(0.0,0.05,m)
    return _normalize_positive(a)
```
Need into np.maximum returns array.

#### `_block_level_score`
```
def _block_level_score(a):
    a=_normalize_positive(a)
    m=a.size
    if m==0: return inf
    c=np.convolve(a,a)
    M=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*m*M)
```

#### `_block_direction`
Direct with weights.

#### `_block_refine`
Use `_block_level_score` for candidates. Need avoid scoring best each loop? We update best_sc.
```
def _block_refine(a, m, rng, deadline):
    a=_normalize_positive(a)
    best=a.copy(); best_sc=_block_level_score(best)
    step=0.2
    alpha=0.08
    stall=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       c=np.convolve(a,a); M=float(c.max())
       if not finite or M<=0: break
       sigma=max(1e-15, alpha*M)
       z=(c-M)/sigma; z=np.clip(z, -30, 0)
       w=np.exp(z); ws=w.sum()
       if ws<=0: w=np.zeros_like(c); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1
       else: w=1/ws*w
       d=_block_direction(a,w)
       dmax=float(d.max())
       if dmax<=0: break
       d=d/dmax
       improved=False
       for mult in (0.25,0.5,1.0,2.0,0.1,4.0):
          st=step*mult
          cand=a*np.exp(-np.clip(st*d,0.0,50.0))
          cand=_normalize_positive(cand)
          sc=_block_level_score(cand)
          if finite and sc<best_sc-1e-12:
             a=cand; best=cand.copy(); best_sc=sc; step=min(3.0,st*1.05); improved=True; break
       if improved:
          stall=0; alpha=max(0.005, alpha*0.997)
       else:
          step*=0.7; stall+=1
          if stall==15: alpha=min(0.5, alpha*1.5)
          if stall>40:
             cand=best*(1.0+rng.normal(0.05,1.0,m)); cand=_normalize_positive(cand)
             a=cand; stall=0; step=0.2
             if _block_level_score(a)>best_sc*1.5:
                 a=best.copy()
       it+=1
       if it%1000==0: print(f"    block m={m} iter {it} best {best_sc:.6f}", flush=True)
    return best, best_sc
```
Note: When perturbing, we set a=cand even if worse, to explore, but keep best. That's okay; if it returns far worse and time ends, we return best. Good.

But `_block_level_score` normalizes each time, so `cand` normalized. OK.

#### `_scipy_block_opt`
Top-level.
```
def _scipy_block_obj(z, m):
    a=_softmax(np.asarray(z,float))
    return _block_level_score(a)

def _scipy_block_opt(m, deadline, rng):
    try:
       from scipy.optimize import differential_evolution
    except Exception:
       return None, np.inf
    if time.monotonic() > deadline: return None, inf
    bounds=[(-3.0,3.0) for _ in range(m)]
    maxiter = 250 if m<=8 else (150 if m<=16 else 80)
    # If deadline tight, reduce
    maxiter = max(20, int(maxiter * min(1.0, max(0.1, (deadline-now)//10.0))) )
    try:
       de = differential_evolution(_scipy_block_obj, bounds, args=(m,), maxiter=maxiter, popsize=max(15, m*2), tol=1e-5, seed=int(rng.integers(0,2**31-1)), initial='latinhypercube', updating='deferred', polish=True)
    except Exception:
       return None, inf
    if hasattr(de,'x') and de.x is not None:
       a=_softmax(de.x); sc=_block_level_score(a)
       return a, sc
    return None, inf
```
`polish=True` may be slow but small. If not available, okay. Need avoid `args=(m,)` if SciPy version? differential_evolution supports args. Good.

```
def _search_block_m(m, N, deadline, rng, interpolation_a=None):
    best_a=None; best_block=np.inf
    # collect candidates
    cands=[]
    if interpolation_a is not None and interpolation_a.size>0:
       cands.append(_resample(interpolation_a, m))
    for i in range(4): cands.append(_random_block_start(m,rng))
    # evaluate and quick refine each under time
    per=max(0.5, (deadline-time.monotonic())/(len(cands)+1))
    for a in cands:
       if time.monotonic()>=deadline: break
       d=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+min(8.0, per))
       a2,sc=_block_refine(a,m,rng,d)
       if finite and sc<best_block: best_block=sc; best_a=a2
    # optional scipy if time > 10
    if time.monotonic() + 5.0 < deadline:
       a3,sc=_scipy_block_opt(m, min(deadline, time.monotonic()+20.0), rng)
       if a3 is not None:
          # refine scipy result little
          d=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+3.0)
          a4,sc4=_block_refine(a3,m,rng,d)
          ...
    # choose exact expanded among best a and maybe resampled? Return best satisfying lower block.
    if best_a is None: best_a=_resample? ../n
    # exact score for expanded
    seq=_expand_blocks(N,best_a)
    exact=_score(seq)
    return best_a, exact
```

```
def _lengths_from_masses(N, masses):
    masses=np.asarray(masses,float); masses=np.maximum(masses,0); s=masses.sum()
    if s<=0 or N<=0: return np.ones(N, dtype=int)
    masses/=s
    raw=N*masses
    base=np.floor(raw).astype(np.int64)
    diff=int(N-base.sum())
    frac=raw-base
    order=np.argsort(-frac)
    i=0
    m=base.size
    while diff>0:
        base[order[i % m]] += 1
        diff -=1; i+=1
    while diff<0:
        # remove from largest base
        j=int(np.argmax(base))
        if base[j]<=0: break
        base[j]-=1; diff+=1
    # ensure each positive mass has at least one position
    pos=masses>1e-12
    if np.any(pos & (base==0)):
        zeros=np.nonzero(pos & (base==0))[0]
        for j in zeros:
           base[j]=1
        # adjust sum
        while base.sum() > N:
           # remove from largest base that can stay at least 1 if pos
           min_len=np.where(pos,1,0)
           candidates=base-min_len
           j=int(np.argmax(candidates))
           if candidates[j] <=0:
              break
           base[j]-=1
        while base.sum() < N:
           j=int(np.argmax(base))
           base[j]+=1
    # if still sum wrong, adjust arbitrary
    while base.sum() < N:
       base[int(np.argmax(base))]+=1
    while base.sum() > N:
       j=int(np.argmax(base)); 
       if base[j]==0: base[:] = np.minimum(base, N?) # unlikely
       else: base[j]-=1
    return base
```
Likely fine.

`_expand_blocks`:
```
def _expand_blocks(N, masses):
    masses=np.asarray(masses,float)
    if masses.size ==0: masses=np.array([1.0])
    lengths=_lengths_from_masses(N, masses)
    masses=_normalize_positive(masses)
    out=np.zeros(N, dtype=float)
    pos=0
    for L, mass in zip(lengths, masses):
       if L>0 and mass>0:
          out[pos:pos+L]=mass/L
          pos+=L
    if pos<N:
       # fill remaining with average of last or small noise
       out[pos:]=out[pos-1] if pos>0 else 0.0
    elif pos>N:
       out=out[:N]
    return _normalize_positive(out)
```
If fill has issue sum not exactly? `_normalize` ensures sum1; but if duplicate last value, slight. Good.

`_lengths_from_masses`: If base.sum()<N and diff <0? Actually floor sum <=N; diff>=0 normally. If due integer weird, diff maybe negative; while diff<0 remove. Good.
When adjusting positive zero mass lengths, if base.sum()>N, candidates = base - pos_min. If all candidates <=0, break leaving sum>N; final while base.sum()>N removes from largest, may reduce positive min below1 leading length0 for positive. But N>=m usually. Rare.

```
if sc_ex < best_exact: best_exact=sc_ex; best_seq=seq.copy()
```

```python
"""
Search for low-peak nonnegative convolution coefficient sequences.

Strategy
--------
The evaluator is scale invariant:
    score(a) = 2*n*max(a * a) / (sum(a)**2)

A very useful way to reduce the peak is to use a short effective support
(realized in the full required length by short uniform blocks) whose
convolution coefficients are nearly flat.  The program:

1. Starts from several constructive/random block-mass vectors.
2. Refines them with a log-sum-exp smoothed multiplicative gradient
   descent on the block-level objective 2*m*max(block convolution).
3. Optionally uses SciPy's differential evolution as a derivative-free
   global corruptor for small block lengths.
4. Expands the best block masses to the required length and applies a full
   sequence multiplicative refinement using FFT-based convolutions.

A fixed fallback is a uniform sequence, but the short-bound block search is
typically enough to beat the 1.5027 target quickly.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

TARGET_SCORE = 1.5027
DEFAULT_LENGTH = 3809


def _normalize_positive(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if a.size == 0:
        return a
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a[a < 0.0] = 0.0
    a[a > 1000.0] = 1000.0
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, a.size)
    return a / s


def _softmax(z):
    z = np.asarray(z, dtype=np.float64)
    if z.size == 0:
        return z
    z = z - float(np.max(z))
    p = np.exp(z)
    s = float(p.sum())
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.ones(z.size, dtype=np.float64) / max(1, z.size)
    return p / s


def _resample(a, n):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    m = a.size
    if n <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if m == 0:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    if m == 1:
        return np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64) / n
    if m == n:
        return _normalize_positive(a)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, endpoint=False)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=False)
    b = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    return _normalize_positive(b)


def _convolution(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 600:
        return np.convolve(a, a)
    need = 2 * n - 1
    L = 1
    while L < need:
        L <<= 1
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    out = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)
    out = out[:need]
    out[~np.isfinite(out)] = 0.0
    out[out < 0.0] = 0.0
    return out


def _score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    c = _convolution(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * n * m / (s * s))


def _block_level_score(a):
    a = _normalize_positive(a)
    m = a.size
    if m == 0:
        return np.inf
    c = np.convolve(a, a)
    mx = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * m * mx)


def _block_direction(a, weights):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    weights = np.asarray(weights, dtype=np.float64)
    m = a.size
    d = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    for k, wk in enumerate(weights):
        if wk == 0.0:
            continue
        i0 = max(0, k - (m - 1))
        i1 = min(m - 1, k)
        # d[i] += wk * a[k-i], for i=i0..i1
        d[i0:i1 + 1] += wk * a[k - i0:k - i1 - 1:-1]
    return d


def _block_refine(a, m, rng, deadline):
    a = _normalize_positive(a)
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = _block_level_score(best)

    step = 0.15
    alpha = 0.10
    stall = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = np.convolve(a, a)
        mx = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 1e-300:
            break

        sigma = max(1e-15, alpha * mx)
        z = (c - mx) / sigma
        z = np.clip(z, -30.0, 0.0)
        w = np.exp(z)
        ws = float(w.sum())
        if ws <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
            w = np.zeros_like(c)
            w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        else:
            w = w / ws

        d = _block_direction(a, w)
        dmax = float(np.max(d))
        if dmax <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(dmax):
            break
        d = d / dmax

        improved = False
        for mult in (0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 0.1, 4.0):
            st = step * mult
            ratios = np.exp(-np.clip(st * d, 0.0, 50.0))
            cand = a * ratios
            cand = _normalize_positive(cand)
            sc = _block_level_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                a = cand
                best = cand.copy()
                best_sc = sc
                step = min(3.0, st * 1.05)
                improved = True
                break

        if improved:
            stall = 0
            alpha = max(0.005, alpha * 0.997)
        else:
            step = max(1e-4, step * 0.7)
            stall += 1
            if stall == 15:
                alpha = min(0.5, alpha * 1.5)
            if stall > 40:
                noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, size=m)
                cand = best * np.exp(noise)
                cand = _normalize_positive(cand)
                a = cand
                stall = 0
                step = 0.2
                scnw = _block_level_score(a)
                if not np.isfinite(scnw) or scnw > best_sc * 1.5:
                    a = best.copy()

        it += 1
        if it % 1000 == 0:
            print(f"    block m={m} iter={it} best={best_sc:.6f}", flush=True)

    return best, best_sc


def _random_block_start(m, rng):
    m = max(1, int(m))
    x = np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)
    center = 0.5 * (m - 1)
    mode = int(rng.integers(0, 12))

    if mode == 0:
        a = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64)
    elif mode == 1:
        a = np.linspace(1.0, 0.1, m, dtype=np.float64)
    elif mode == 2:
        a = np.linspace(0.1, 1.0, m, dtype=np.float64)
    elif mode == 3:
        phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        amp = float(rng.choice([0.3, 0.6, 0.9, 1.2]))
        a = 1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * x / m + phase)
    elif mode == 4:
        phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        amp = float(rng.choice([0.3, 0.6, 0.9, 1.2]))
        a = 1.0 + amp * np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x / m + phase)
    elif mode == 5:
        h = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
        for i in range(m // 2):
            v = float(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0))
            h[i] = v
            h[m - 1 - i] = -v
        scale = float(np.linalg.norm(h) * np.sqrt(max(1, m)))
        if scale > 0.0:
            h = h * (8.0 / scale)
        amp = float(rng.choice([0.2, 0.4, 0.7, 1.0]))
        a = 1.0 + amp * h
    elif mode == 6:
        sigma = m * float(rng.uniform(0.15, 0.45))
        g = (x - center) / max(1e-6, sigma)
        h = g * np.exp(-g * g)
        amp = float(rng.choice([0.3, 0.6, 1.0]))
        a = 1.0 + amp * h
    elif mode == 7:
        alpha_shape = float(rng.choice([0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 3.0, 8.0]))
        a = rng.gamma(alpha_shape, np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64))
    elif mode == 8:
        alpha_vec = np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64) * float(rng.choice([0.2, 0.5, 1.0, 3.0, 10.0]))
        a = rng.dirichlet(alpha_vec)
    else:
        a = np.maximum(0.1, rng.normal(1.0, 0.4, size=m))

    a = np.maximum(1e-4, np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64))
    if float(rng.random()) < 0.2:
        a = a + rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, size=m)
    return _normalize_positive(a)


def _lengths_from_masses(N, masses):
    masses = np.asarray(masses, dtype=np.float64)
    masses = np.maximum(masses, 0.0)
    s = float(masses.sum())
    if N <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.int64)
    if s <= 0.0 or masses.size == 0:
        return np.ones(N, dtype=np.int64)
    masses = masses / s

    raw = N * masses
    base = np.floor(raw).astype(np.int64)
    diff = int(N - base.sum())
    frac = raw - base
    order = np.argsort(-frac)
    i = 0
    m = base.size

    while diff > 0 and m > 0:
        base[order[i % m]] += 1
        diff -= 1
        i += 1

    while diff < 0:
        j = int(np.argmax(base))
        if base[j] <= 0:
            break
        base[j] -= 1
        diff += 1

    pos = masses > 1e-12
    if np.any(pos & (base == 0)):
        zeros = np.nonzero(pos & (base == 0))[0]
        for j in zeros:
            base[j] = 1

        min_len = np.where(pos, 1, 0).astype(np.int64)
        while base.sum() > N:
            candidates = base - min_len
            j = int(np.argmax(candidates))
            if candidates[j] <= 0:
                break
            base[j] -= 1

        while base.sum() < N:
            j = int(np.argmax(base))
            base[j] += 1

    while base.sum() < N:
        j = int(np.argmax(base))
        base[j] += 1
    while base.sum() > N:
        j = int(np.argmax(base))
        if base[j] <= 0:
            break
        base[j] -= 1

    return base


def _expand_blocks(N, masses):
    masses = np.asarray(masses, dtype=np.float64)
    if masses.size == 0:
        masses = np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if N <= 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    lengths = _lengths_from_masses(N, masses)
    masses = _normalize_positive(masses)

    out = np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64)
    pos = 0
    for L, mass in zip(lengths, masses):
        if L > 0 and mass > 0.0:
            out[pos:pos + L] = mass / float(L)
            pos += L

    if pos < N:
        fill = out[pos - 1] if pos > 0 else 0.0
        out[pos:] = fill
    elif pos > N:
        out = out[:N]

    return _normalize_positive(out)


def _direction_from_weights(a, weights):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    weights = np.asarray(weights, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)
    rb = weights[::-1].copy()
    need = n + rb.size - 1
    L = 1
    while L < need:
        L <<= 1
    FA = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    FB = np.fft.rfft(rb, L)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(FA * FB, L)
    conv = conv[:need]
    conv[~np.isfinite(conv)] = 0.0
    # g[i] = sum_j a[j] * weights[i+j]
    return conv[n - 1:2 * n - 1][::-1].copy()


def _full_refine(a, rng, deadline):
    a = _normalize_positive(a)
    best = a.copy()
    best_sc = _score(best)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return best, np.inf

    step = 0.08
    alpha = 0.08
    stall = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = _convolution(a)
        mx = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 1e-300:
            break

        sigma = max(1e-15, alpha * mx)
        z = (c - mx) / sigma
        z = np.clip(z, -30.0, 0.0)
        w = np.exp(z)
        ws = float(w.sum())
        if ws <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
            w = np.zeros_like(c)
            w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        else:
            w = w / ws

        d = _direction_from_weights(a, w)
        dmax = float(np.max(d))
        if dmax <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(dmax):
            break
        d = d / dmax

        improved = False
        for mult in (0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 0.1, 4.0):
            st = step * mult
            ratios = np.exp(-np.clip(st * d, 0.0, 50.0))
            cand = a * ratios
            cand = _normalize_positive(cand)
            sc = _score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                a = cand
                best = cand.copy()
                best_sc = sc
                step = min(3.0, st * 1.05)
                improved = True
                break

        if improved:
            stall = 0
            alpha = max(0.005, alpha * 0.998)
        else:
            step = max(1e-4, step * 0.7)
            stall += 1
            if stall == 20:
                alpha = min(0.35, alpha * 1.5)
            if stall > 60:
                noise = rng.normal(0.0, 0.05, size=n)
                cand = best * np.exp(noise)
                cand = _normalize_positive(cand)
                a = cand
                stall = 0
                step = 0.1
                scnw = _score(a)
                if not np.isfinite(scnw) or scnw > best_sc * 1.5:
                    a = best.copy()

        it += 1
        if it % 200 == 0:
            print(f"    full iter={it} best={best_sc:.6f}", flush=True)

    return best, best_sc


def _scipy_block_obj(z, m):
    a = _softmax(z)
    return _block_level_score(a)


def _scipy_block_opt(m, deadline, rng):
    try:
        from scipy.optimize import differential_evolution
    except Exception:
        return None, np.inf

    if m <= 0:
        return None, np.inf
    if time.monotonic() > deadline:
        return None, np.inf

    bounds = []
    for _ in range(m):
        bounds.append((-3.0, 3.0))

    if m <= 8:
        maxiter = 250
    elif m <= 16:
        maxiter = 160
    else:
        maxiter = 80

    # Do not over-invest in SciPy if the remaining budget is small.
    frac = float(max(0.0, min(1.0, (deadline - time.monotonic()) / 12.0)))
    maxiter = max(20, int(maxiter * frac))

    seed = int(rng.integers(0, 2**31 - 1))
    try:
        de = differential_evolution(
            _scipy_block_obj,
            bounds,
            args=(m,),
            maxiter=maxiter,
            popsize=max(15, 2 * m),
            tol=1e-5,
            seed=seed,
            initial="latinhypercube",
            updating="deferred",
            polih=True,
            polish=True,
        )
    except Exception:
        try:
            de = differential_evolution(
                _scipy_block_obj,
                bounds,
                args=(m,),
                maxiter=maxiter,
                popsize=max(15, 2 * m),
                tol=1e-5,
                seed=seed,
                initial="latinhypercube",
                polish=False,
            )
        except Exception:
            return None, np.inf

    try:
        if getattr(de, "x", None) is not None:
            a = _softmax(de.x)
            sc = _block_level_score(a)
            return a, sc
    except Exception:
        pass

    return None, np.inf


def _search_block_m(m, N, deadline, rng, interpolation_a=None):
    m = max(1, int(m))
    if m > N:
        m = max(1, int(N))

    best_block_a = None
    best_block_sc = np.inf
    best_seq = None
    best_seq_sc = np.inf

    cands = []
    if interpolation_a is not None and np.asarray(interpolation_a).size > 0:
        cands.append(_resample(interpolation_a, m))
    for _ in range(4):
        cands.append(_random_block_start(m, rng))

    now = time.monotonic()
    per = max(0.8, min(6.0, (deadline - now) / max(1, len(cands) + 1)))

    for a0 in cands:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break
        d_end = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + per)
        a1, sc_block = _block_refine(a0, m, rng, d_end)
        if np.isfinite(sc_block) and sc_block < best_block_sc:
            best_block_sc = sc_block
            best_block_a = a1.copy()

        a_check = a1 if a1 is not None else a0
        seq = _expand_blocks(N, a_check)
        sc_seq = _score(seq)
        if np.isfinite(sc_seq) and sc_seq < best_seq_sc:
            best_seq_sc = sc_seq
            best_seq = seq.copy()

    # Optional derivative-free global search.  Keep it limited.
    if m <= 16 and time.monotonic() + 4.0 < deadline:
        a_de, sc_de = _scipy_block_opt(m, min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 18.0), rng)
        if a_de is not None:
            a_de, sc_de2 = _block_refine(a_de, m, rng, min(deadline, time.monotonic() + 3.0))
            sc_de = min(sc_de, sc_de2)
            if np.isfinite(sc_de) and sc_de < best_block_sc:
                best_block_sc = sc_de
                best_block_a = a_de.copy()
            seq = _expand_blocks(N, a_de)
            sc_seq = _score(seq)
            if np.isfinite(sc_seq) and sc_seq < best_seq_sc:
                best_seq_sc = sc_seq
                best_seq = seq.copy()

    if best_seq is None:
        a0 = _normalize_positive(np.ones(m, dtype=np.float64))
        best_block_a = a0
        best_block_sc = _block_level_score(a0)
        best_seq = _expand_blocks(N, a0)
        best_seq_sc = _score(best_seq)

    return best_block_a, best_seq, best_seq_sc


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    budget_s = max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = start_time + budget_s - 4.0

    N = DEFAULT_LENGTH
    try:
        init = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if init.ndim == 1 and init.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(init)):
            N = int(init.size)
    except Exception:
        N = DEFAULT_LENGTH

    if N <= 0:
        N = DEFAULT_LENGTH

    # Safe fallback: uniform.
    best_seq = np.ones(N, dtype=np.float64) / N
    best_score = _score(best_seq)
    print(f"[start] N={N} score={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Block-mass search.  This is the main cheap structural search.
    m_list = [5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 12, 16, 24]
    unique_ms = []
    for mm in m_list:
        mm = min(int(mm), N)
        if mm >= 1 and mm not in unique_ms:
            unique_ms.append(mm)

    block_deadline = min(deadline, start_time + 180.0)
    interp_a = None

    for m in unique_ms:
        if time.monotonic() >= block_deadline:
            break
        print(f"[search] block m={m}", flush=True)
        a_mas, seq, seq_sc = _search_block_m(
            m, N, min(block_deadline, time.monotonic() + 30.0), rng, interp_a
        )
        if np.isfinite(seq_sc) and seq_sc < best_score:
            best_seq = seq.copy()
            best_score = seq_sc
            interp_a = a_mas.copy()
            print(f"[search] block m={m} exact best={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)

        if best_score < 1.25:
            # Diminishing returns; stop block search and polish fully.
            break
        if best_score < 1.35 and m >= 16:
            break

    # Full-length multiplicative polishing.
    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 30.0 and N > 1:
        print(f"[polish] full refinement, remaining={remaining:.1f}s", flush=True)
        full_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + max(60.0, remaining - 5.0))
        seq2, sc2 = _full_refine(best_seq, rng, full_deadline)
        if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_score:
            best_seq = seq2.copy()
            best_score = sc2
            print(f"[polish] full best={best_score:.6f}", flush=True)

    # Final authoritative score check using the evaluator if available.
    out_list = [float(x) for x in best_seq]
    final_score = best_score
    try:
        final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(out_list))
    except Exception:
        final_score = best_score

    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        # Last-resort valid fallback.
        out_list = [1.0 / N] * N
        try:
            final_score = float(evaluate_sequence(out_list))
        except Exception:
            final_score = 2.0

    print(f"[final] length={len(out_list)} score={final_score:.6f}", flush=True)
    return out_list
```
